Separately, wordpress-org-news (added by the previous audit specifically to close the discovery gap that caused the WP2Shell miss) was never fetched once in eight runs, its last_successful_fetch still null, because candidates are absent from the digest's active_ids and the allocation rule never said they rotate.
    3. Per-fact attribution is the pipeline's dominant residual defect class. 11 of the audit's 25 truth findings were "true fact, wrong cited source" (9 imprecisions plus 2 of the 3 factual errors), spread across operational entries and the weekly, surviving verifier loops of 3–8 iterations. The v3.26 weekly fix proved the pattern: its mechanically-checkable half (citation dates) went from 12-of-15 entries drifting to 0 of 52 citations wrong; its judgement-call half (does this source carry this fact) stayed flat at 4 defects per batch.
    4. PD-11(b)'s "otherwise requiring an out-of-band response" limb had collapsed to "exploited or nothing": two consecutive audits recovered unexploited flaws whose mechanics forced the timeline (2026-07-18: Moodle local_o365 JWT forgery; 2026-07-26: Balbooa Gridbox cookie-forgery to Joomla Super User). The watch item opened on 07-18 set exactly this condition for shipping a clarification.
    5. A memory-only rule that did not reach composition. An OT/ICS entry shipped five CVEs with fixed: "not stated in advisory" when the advisory's own CSAF remediation block and the linked GHSA both named the fixed release, the guidance existed in .claude/memory/csaf-msrc-transcription.md but not in the prompt.

    What changed

    • prompts/cti-run.md PD-13 restructured into the deadline half (unchanged: never justifies priority, never opens an update entry, never frames an action) and the listing half (a KEV addition that moves an already-covered CVE from not-confirmed-exploited to confirmed-exploited is an exploitation-status change and ships as an update_of delta; adding cisa-kev to a CVE already described as exploited ships nothing). New duty: before dropping a KEV addition as already-covered, re-read the covered entry's own cves[].status and summary rather than trusting recall of it.
    • prompts/cti-run.md Phase 0 allocation: new text in rule 2 (status: candidate records rotate with their domain's standard tier; absent/null last_successful_fetch sorts oldest, so a source added last run tops the next slice; active_ids is not the allocation list) and new rule 4 (act on sources.promotion_due from the digest, flip to active, record in sources_changed[]). Phase 5 lifecycle line now points at the digest for the count.
    • tools/run_summary.py: new sources.promotion_due[] in the digest ({id, contributing_runs, last_run_id}): counts distinct run_ids among published entries whose frontmatter sources[] cites each candidate's host or a subdomain of it, threshold via --promote-after (default 3). This is what makes the promotion rule executable by a stateless fire.
    • prompts/cti-run.md Phase 4 item 5: per-fact attribution gains two mechanical habits: one citation per clause, not per sentence (a trailing citation silently claims the whole sentence), and never chain two distinct vulnerabilities, CVEs or incidents inside one identifier-labelled clause.
    • Both verifier definitions (lockstep): truth check 2 gains (d) a per-citation adjacency check, explicitly not a sample: for every inline citation, confirm that page carries each fact in the clause it terminates, with the two recurring shapes named (detail belonging to the other co-cited source; count or as-of date spliced onto another figure's date) and identifier-mismatch binding called out; and (e) citation date vs the source's own publication metadata, with a 1-day UTC-rendering tolerance.
    • prompts/weekly-summary.md: the v3.26 duty carries a recorded effectiveness verdict (limb (a) took, limb (b) did not) and limb (b) is now mechanical: one citation per clause, no two-vulnerability chaining under one identifier, and quotation marks mean a contiguous verbatim substring.
    • prompts/cti-run.md PD-11(b): the "otherwise" limb is defended by name: a flaw with no exploitation signal still clears (b) when its own mechanics force the timeline (anonymous single-request path to full administrative control of internet-facing infrastructure; a trivially-rediscoverable bug whose fix diff hands over the technique; a disclosure whose sibling flaws in the same wave were weaponised within days), stated in one clause. "Absence of exploitation is not evidence of safety when the exploit is a cookie value."
    • prompts/cti-run.md Phase 2 step 4: read affected / fixed from structured fields (CSAF product_status, remediations[].vendor_fix, or the vendor's version table), never the prose summary.

    What stays

    PD-13's substance is intact; foreign-jurisdiction compliance deadlines remain non-signal, and this changes nothing about priority (a KEV listing still never justifies critical/high by itself; the recovered WP2Shell delta ships at high on exploitation and exposure, not on the KEV flag). PD-11's conservatism is unchanged: relevance doubt still resolves toward drop, the routine patch cycle is still out of scope, and the clarified limb narrows rather than widens what qualifies by demanding a named mechanical reason. Volume stays governed by the gate with no target or ceiling. The source lifecycle's thresholds, the one-new-candidate-per-run cap, the never-demote-on-403 rule and append-only notes are untouched; only the counting is now mechanical. Verifier scope, F-code vocabulary, return contract, 30-min cap, rotation and the double-CLEAN publish gate (cap 8) are unchanged; the adjacency check tightens F3's existing definition rather than adding a category. All hard invariants hold. quality-audit.md moves to v3.29 in lockstep with no body change.

    3.28, 2026-07-18 (zero-warning discipline: runs fix their own warnings, the weekly audit sweeps the store to zero; acknowledgment ledger for settled history; renderer emphasis-leak fixes)

    Why

    Operator-directed, same day as v3.27: the store sat at 8 standing check_run.py --all WARNs (3 pre-watchdog runaway durations, 5 era-correct confirmation waivers from the 5-cap era) plus 2 site/build.py self-check warnings, all "pre-existing", none anyone's job to clear. The operator's directive: the repo must be perfectly clean; warnings are fixed, not tolerated; every run ensures its own warnings are fixed, and at the latest the weekly audit fixes them all, acting as a weekly cleanup that repairs everything possible. The blocker was structural: warnings on immutable history (a published record's duration_seconds, a recorded waiver) literally cannot be fixed without falsifying records, so they accumulated forever and buried real signal.

    What changed

    • Acknowledgment ledger (state/warning_acknowledgments.json + tools/check_run.py): the audit-reviewed escape hatch for genuinely unfixable warnings. warn() reclassifies a warning as ACK when a ledger record's check matches the label and its match (≥12 chars, pinned to the specific run/subject) is a substring of the message; acknowledged warnings print in their own section with the recorded reason and count as zero (summary: … · N acknowledged). Malformed/unreadable ledger records FAIL (ack-ledger). Seeded with the 8 reviewed acknowledgments (3 runaways root-caused in the 07-11 audit and answered by the v3.21 watchdog; 5 pre-v3.27 cap waivers answered by the v3.27 cap raise). Discipline: only the weekly audit (or an operator-directed session) adds records; a run never self-acknowledges its own fresh warnings.
    • prompts/cti-run.md Phase 5.5, zero-warning duty: before commit a run fixes every warning it caused or can fix; only its own telemetry facts (explained in the notes) and settled prior history may survive, for the audit to sweep.
    • prompts/quality-audit.md; the audit is the pipeline's weekly cleanup: mission statement extended; new Phase 3 item 8 (warning sweep to zero: every --all WARN and every build self-check warning is a mandatory Phase 4 work item; the audit does not publish until --all ends 0 warn · 0 fail and the build is warning-free; dead ledger rows are pruned); new Phase 4 fix class (acknowledge-on-immutable-history, disclosed in the report, never as a substitute for a possible fix); report §7, quality-gate checklist, and the Output block now carry the sweep (warnings: 0 open (N acknowledged) · build self-check: clean). Beyond warnings, the audit ships every small repair it finds (renderer defects, drifted docs, dead recipes) inside § META authority.
    • site/build.py, the two live feed warnings fixed at root cause: (a) new _strip_md_emphasis() replaces the five bare .strip("*") headline cleanups that half-stripped a leading bold token (**Avalon** framework … → Avalon** framework … in TL;DR bullets, aria-labels and feeds); (b) evidence quotes (entry-cite__quote) render inline emphasis instead of escaping it raw (a GHSA quote carrying **Root access** now renders bold); (c) _inline_text() drops ** that survives rendering and a field-leading orphan *; unbalanced markers in immutable source fields are syntax, not prose (fixes the 06-23 Gitea summary artifact); (d) the feed self-check now flags ANY post-scrub ** (paired or orphaned), closing the gap that let the half-stripped class through.
    • prompts/check-run-fixes.md, CLAUDE.md, docs/operating.md: WARN recipes reframed under the zero-warning discipline; new hard-rule paragraph, file-map entry, and operator-signal row (a WARN outliving an audit = skipped sweep; a growing ledger = acknowledging instead of fixing).

    What stays

    Warning checks themselves are untouched, nothing is silenced at the check level; the ledger only reclassifies specific, reviewed, immutable-history instances, each visible in the output with its reason and in the commit diff. Exit-code semantics unchanged (FAILs block, warnings never did; the discipline is prompt-level duty, not a new gate). Entry and run-record immutability hold: zero content files were edited; both fixes are renderer/tooling-side. The mechanical gate, the double-CLEAN loop (cap 8, v3.27), feature-branch publishing, and all hard invariants unchanged. weekly-summary.md moves to v3.28 in lockstep with no body change (it executes the intel run's Phase 5.5 verbatim, inheriting the duty).

    3.27, 2026-07-18 (operator directives on the 07-18 audit: verifier iteration cap 5 → 8; scheduler cadence declared operator-owned and variable; operator watch-item closures are final)

    Why

    Operator-directed, in same-day response to the 2026-07-18 weekly quality audit (docs/audits/2026-07-18-weekly-quality-audit.md, operator response recorded as an addendum there). Three decisions: (1) the audit's recommendation 2 documented that half the post-v3.23 runs were publishing on confirmation_waived at the 5-iteration cap; the double-CLEAN confirmation gate stays required, but the loop gets room to converge instead of churning into the fail-open; (2) the audit's recommendation 1 flagged the post-07-14 drop to a single daily fire as a possibly-dead scheduler slot; the operator confirmed it is intended, and made the general policy explicit: cadence is tuned at will, the pipeline works off the gap to the last run, and a cadence change is never a defect; (3) the bd.zh.ch MedusaLocker watch item is closed by operator decision; the normal run pipeline, not audit tracking, owns any future development of that story.

    What changed

    • Phase 5.7 verifier iteration cap 5 → 8, everywhere normative: cti-run.md (anti-crash guard #2 rationale, decision rule 6 "Iteration 8 without a publishable outcome", loop-shape bullet "Cap 8 iterations", hard-invariant checklist item 11 "≤8 iterations"); weekly-summary.md and quality-audit.md shared-machinery references ("cap 8"); prompts/verification.md (preflight checklist ≤8, gate paragraph, loop heading); both verifier definitions in lockstep (frontmatter description, gatekeeper block "capped at 8 iterations", padding rule "8-iteration ceiling", context-budget note, loop-contract "Hard cap 8 iterations"); tools/check_run.py VERIFIER_ITERATION_CAP 5 → 8 with a version gate (CAP_EIGHT_FROM = (3, 27)) so the "first CLEAN landed at the cap" waiver check keeps 5-cap semantics for pre-v3.27 records; site/build.py cap-breach tooltip de-hardcoded ("at the final iteration" instead of "at iteration 5"); CLAUDE.md, README.md, docs/routines.md, docs/architecture.md. The double-CLEAN gate itself is unchanged, two consecutive CLEAN verdicts on two different models still gate every CLEAN publish; only the safety-valve ceiling moved.
    • Scheduler cadence policy (quality-audit.md Phase 3 telemetry item; cti-run.md runtime banner; CLAUDE.md): the cadence is operator-owned and intentionally variable, more fires, fewer fires, different slots, changed at will. The pipeline is gap-driven (PD-7, unchanged; it already did this); what the audit checks is that the gap-derived windows self-healed across a change with no coverage hole. A cadence change is NOT a finding and NOT an operator recommendation; only a record-less gap on a schedule that was supposed to fire remains an availability signal. Memory: .claude/memory/scheduler-and-workflow-races.md + index carry the decision.
    • Operator watch-item closures are final (quality-audit.md Phase 0 step 3): a watch item the operator explicitly closed (audit-report operator-response addendum, or a dated memory note) gets no re-check duty and is never re-opened by an audit; new information on the underlying story flows through the normal intel runs (as update_of where an entry exists). Applied: bd.zh.ch (Kanton Zürich Baudirektion MedusaLocker listing) closed in the 07-18 report's addendum, open watch-item ledger drops to 3 + 1 fix-effectiveness check.

    What stays

    The double-CLEAN publish gate (v3.23) in full: rotation, confirmation pass on the other model, confirmation_waived bookkeeping, low-residual early exit, verification_residual_count semantics, ≥1 iteration always / ≥2 for a CLEAN publish. The 30-min verifier and 45-min research caps. PD-7's gap-derived window formula, untouched; this release only names the policy it already implements. Entry immutability (the closed watch item's entry and registry record stay as published), F1–F18, the mechanical gate, feature-branch-only publishing, relevance discipline, run-record-per-fire, memory commits. Pre-v3.27 run records stay green (version-gated cap check, same pattern as v3.18/v3.21/v3.23). All three master prompts move to v3.27 in lockstep; both verifier bodies regenerated byte-identically.

    3.26, 2026-07-18 (weekly citation-date + per-fact attribution duty at synthesis time)

    Why

    Shipped by the 2026-07-18 weekly quality audit (runs/2026-07-18/2026-07-18T1208Z-audit.md). The audit's retrospective truth pass over the 15 W28 strategic entries found a batch-level pattern the operational entries do not share: 12 of 15 weekly entries carried at least one primary citation dated 1–8 days later than the source's actual publication date (the pipeline's discovery/processing date had been reused as the citation date), and four entries attributed a specific fact to a co-cited source that does not carry it, a Langflow KEV-listing claim cited to a Sysdig post that predates the KEV add and says the opposite; a CERT.LV "unpatched for roughly two years" detail that is The Record's, uncited; Unit 42's "580 victims across 77 countries" figure spliced onto the as-of date of an adjacent chart ("through July 3" vs the figure's own "Through July 7"); and a "The Syndicate" attribution cited to a Nayax SEC filing that never names the group. All underlying facts were true; the defect class is synthesis-time citation drift (F3), which the weekly's re-framing workflow invites because it composes from operational entries and findings rather than fresh fetches.

    What changed

    • prompts/weekly-summary.md Phase 4, new composition bullet: citation dates and per-fact attribution are re-verified at weekly composition, never inherited from pipeline bookkeeping. Inline citation dates come from the source's own publication metadata (fetched this run, or verbatim from the referenced operational entry's sources[] record), never from discovered_at or findings timestamps; every number, as-of date, and status claim is cited to the specific source that states it, and a fact carried only by an uncited secondary is either fetched-and-cited or dropped. Verifier flags both patterns as F3.

    What stays

    The four defective W28 entries stay as published, entry immutability holds; none of the defects changes a defender decision, all are documented in docs/audits/2026-07-18-weekly-quality-audit.md (no update entries warranted, following the 2026-07-11 minor-findings precedent). The weekly's lens, W-PD-1, references mechanism, section shapes, and all shared machinery are untouched. cti-run.md and quality-audit.md move to v3.26 in lockstep with no body change.

    3.25, 2026-07-18 (jina reader demoted to LAST-RESORT transport; anonymous free tier no longer assumed available; reader-pool health institutionalized in the quality audit)

    Why

    Operator-directed, triggered by the 2026-07-18T0409Z run: the jina reader key pool exhausted mid-window (HTTP 402 on the configured key and HTTP 401 on the anonymous free tier, which the 2026-07-13 credential ladder had assumed was an always-available backstop, "degrades fidelity, never availability" turned out to be false). Coverage survived only because the direct-transport fallbacks happened to work. Two lessons: (1) the reader is a metered, externally-billed dependency and the v3.8 ladder had it at rung 3 (above the free direct bridge) so routine fetches burned paid credit that direct transports would have served, draining the pool that the genuinely reader-dependent hosts (heise article bodies, cisa.gov dynamic paths, ccn-cert geo-gate, JS-only SPAs) need to stay reachable; (2) nothing in the scheduled pipeline watched the pool balance, so exhaustion was discovered by a run failing into it rather than by an audit predicting it.

    What changed

    • Fetch ladder reordered; jina is rung 4 of 4, the LAST RESORT (.claude/agents/cti-research.md § Fetch tooling reference + § fetch_method + the WebFetch-escalation/completeness/redirect notes; tools/fetch_source.py module docs): RSS feed → direct WebFetch → direct bridge (url <URL> raw body / structured publisher recipe) → jina reader. Full-technical-detail reads now prefer url <URL> (raw body, nothing summarised away) over the reader; the reader is forced directly only when rungs 1–3 failed or the source record pins fetch_method: jina (a pinned record means the host is proven to need it, going straight to the reader there is correct, not a violation). url's built-in auto-fallback to the reader is unchanged, so blocked hosts still recover without switching commands.
    • prompts/cti-run.md Phase 4 deep-read no longer prefers the reader: url <URL> / structured recipe first, heavy raw HTML extracted on disk under work/<run-id>/ (the 2026-07-18 run's proven path), reader only for reader-required hosts. Phase 5 needs-bridge playbook reordered: structured feed → mirror → reader LAST, and fetch_method: jina is pinned only when no direct transport reaches the content.
    • tools/fetch_source.py, anonymous tier no longer assumed: a 401/402 on the anonymous rung is now non-retryable (previously burned 3 attempts + backoff per fetch on a rung observed to be refusing us); docstrings, the exhausted-pool jina-usage warning, and the operator docs now say an exhausted pool can mean a reader outage, not a fidelity downgrade. Removed the _check_host back-compat shim and the ignored ncsc-nl csaf legacy [VERSION] argument (no-backwards-compat policy).
    • prompts/quality-audit.md Phase 3, new ops-hygiene item 4 "Reader-pool health": every weekly audit runs jina-usage; a low/dead pool or repeated 402/401 rotation notes in the window's run records becomes an operator recommendation before exhaustion hits a live run (institutionalizes the memory note that had never made it into the prompt).
    • Both verifier definitions (bodies regenerated in lockstep): the URL-truth escalation now names the reader as the last-resort rung after WebFetch and url, with the ladder parenthetical reordered (structured bridge recipe → jina reader).
    • CLAUDE.md + docs/operating.md + .claude/memory/source-fetch-blocks.md: ladder order, last-resort rule, and the anonymous-tier observation recorded; operating.md reframes JINA_API_KEYS from "recommended" to required-for-the-last-resort-rung and no longer promises anonymous availability.

    What stays

    The reader itself, its credential-ladder rotation, multi-key pool, local response cache, X-Cache-Tolerance, browser-engine tier, and every dedicated bridge recipe are untouched; this release changes when the reader is used and what the pipeline assumes about its free tier, not how it works. fetch_method: jina records (ccn-cert-es, reliaquest, mysites-guru) keep the reader as their working recipe by design. Every hard invariant: no IOCs, two-source verification with carve-outs, entry immutability + update_of, the mechanical gate, the double-CLEAN verifier loop, feature-branch-only publishing, relevance discipline, run-record-per-fire, memory commits. weekly-summary.md moves to v3.25 in lockstep with no body change.

    3.24, 2026-07-13 (audit runs first-class: kind: audit; Ops dashboard tracks the double-CLEAN gate; run selection = the run-log table)

    Why

    Operator-directed, in three parts. (1) Audit fires were recorded as kind: intel with only the -audit run-id suffix distinguishing them (the v3.22 contract), so every consumer that keys on kind (the Ops dashboard's kind pills and intel/weekly counts, sub-agent slot layouts, day-page grouping) silently misrendered them as intel runs, and the essential-coverage gate applied intel-cadence duties to retrospective passes. (2) The v3.23 double-CLEAN publish gate shipped without an operator surface: the dashboard's clean-rate, verdict pills and stacks still counted raw iterations, which would have painted every confirmed two-iteration run as "remediated" and made the confirmation invisible. (3) The Ops run-log's jump-to selector duplicated what the table itself should do; select runs.

    What changed

    • kind: audit is first-class. site/content_model.py RUN_KINDS = (intel, weekly, audit); prompts/quality-audit.md run-record contract now kind: audit (the run-id -audit suffix stays as the human-readable mirror); docs/pipeline.md § run ids + schema comment updated. The two pre-v3.24 audit records (2026-07-11T1435Z-audit, 2026-07-12T1308Z-audit) were migrated in place to kind: audit, an operator-directed record fix, not an entry edit. tools/check_run.py exempts audit runs from the essential-coverage duty (retrospective passes are not source slices). Rendering: audit runs get their own count in the Ops KPI splits, their ad-hoc retrospective-pass keys render as the sub-agent cards/columns (no synthetic "absent" S1–S4), render_run_note headers name non-intel kinds, and stood-down fires surface a head pill instead of an empty grid.
    • Ops double-CLEAN tracking (site/build.py). New helpers _verification_confirmation(run) (classifies v3.23+ runs: confirmed / same-model / waived / single / residual, reads subagent_type first) and _verification_fix_rounds(run) (NEEDS_FIXES iterations, the real remediation count, so a defect-free confirmed run is never painted as remediated). Surfaces: run-log Verif pills (clean ×2, N↻ clean ×2, clean · unconfirmed, clean ×2 same-model, residual unchanged), a leading confirmation chip in every run panel's verification row (✓ double-CLEAN · models / waiver reason), the clean-rate KPI notes double-confirmed count, a new Double-CLEAN gate KPI tile (confirmed/gated · residual publishes · fail-opens), the verification stacks chart counts fix rounds, and entries_dropped_by_verification > 0 is now shown. New run-log Pub column surfaces Phase 7 publish follow-through (ok / main-only / pending).
    • Run selection = the run-log table. The jump-to <select> (and _ops_run_picker_label, its app.js wiring, its CSS) is removed; the Run cell now shows the run id itself linking to /runs/<run-id>/. Legacy /ops/#run=<id> deep links still redirect via a data-runs-base marker. Tests updated in site/test_build.py.

    What stays

    The v3.23 double-CLEAN gate semantics and check_run.py enforcement are untouched, this release only surfaces them. Run-id format, run-record schema (no new required fields), entry immutability, the audit prompt's phases and invariants, and every other consumer of runs/** are unchanged; pre-v3.24 non-audit records render exactly as before. cti-run.md and weekly-summary.md move to v3.24 in lockstep with no body change.

    3.23, 2026-07-13 (double-CLEAN publish gate: two consecutive CLEAN verdicts from two different models; per-run detail pages surface verification & coverage notes)

    Why

    Operator-directed. The Phase 5.7 publish gate rested on a single verdict: whichever model happened to run the iteration that returned CLEAN decided publication alone, so one model's blind spot (the very thing the opus/sonnet rotation exists to catch on the NEEDS_FIXES path) was never challenged on the CLEAN path; an iteration-1 CLEAN published without any second model ever reading the run. The fix makes the rotation carry the publish decision too: a CLEAN is a hypothesis until the next iteration (by rotation always the other model) independently confirms it. Alongside, the operator asked for the run record's verification & coverage notes (borderline drops, judged-not-relevant items, dedup decisions, coverage gaps) to be reachable directly from the Live view, so a reader can check any day what a run dropped and why, without digging through the repo.

    What changed

    • Phase 5.7 publish gate (cti-run.md): the gate to publish is now a confirmed CLEAN, two consecutive iterations, on two different models, both returning verdict CLEAN. Decision rules rewritten: rule 1 publishes only a confirmed CLEAN; new rule 2 spawns the other-model confirmation pass after any unconfirmed CLEAN (it reads cold, carries full verdict weight, and a NEEDS_FIXES from it re-enters the fix loop, restarting the CLEAN chain); the low-residual early exit (now rule 5) and the 5-iteration cap (rule 6) stay the fail-open valves. A first CLEAN landing exactly at the cap publishes unconfirmed as a recorded fail-open. Never spawn the same definition twice in a row; a genuinely blocked other-model spawn (retry first, 2026-06-05 classifier-block precedent) may confirm same-model as a recorded exception.
    • New run-record field verification.confirmation_waived (optional string, docs/pipeline.md § Run records): set ONLY when a run publishes on a CLEAN no second model confirmed (single CLEAN at cap, watchdog-overrun single iteration per anti-crash guard #10, blocked spawn), the reason, verbatim.
    • tools/check_run.py, new verification-confirmation check (v3.23+ records): an unconfirmed final CLEAN FAILs the pre-commit gate unless confirmation_waived or the cap explains it (→ WARN); a same-model confirmation WARNs; NEEDS_FIXES finals (early-exit/cap residual path) are untouched. --all re-checks store-wide at WARN severity (immutable history). Constants DOUBLE_CLEAN_FROM = (3, 23), VERIFIER_ITERATION_CAP = 5.
    • Both verifier definitions (byte-identical bodies regenerated): gatekeeper section now explains the two CLEAN signatures (first CLEAN vs confirming CLEAN) and that a confirmation pass is not a rubber stamp; verdict block and loop paragraph updated; frontmatter descriptions updated in both.
    • Spawn-message contract: a confirmation pass after a CLEAN passes no deltas (only the fact that the previous iteration was CLEAN with zero findings) so the second model anchors on the run's output, not the first model's judgement.
    • prompts/verification.md: gate sentence, rotation paragraph, checklist line and loop heading updated to the double-CLEAN contract.
    • Weekly + quality-audit prompts: banner bumps in lockstep; their Phase 5.7 references updated (weekly's machinery recap line; audit's loop-scope line now reads "≥2 for a CLEAN publish").
    • Site, per-run detail pages /runs/<run-id>/ (site/build.py render_run_detail_page + run_url_path): every run record gets a permanent page carrying the full Ops telemetry panel (sub-agent allocation, per-iteration verification verdicts + findings, sources changed, coverage gaps, bridge invocations) plus the record's narrative body; the verification & coverage notes where borderline drops and judged-not-relevant items live. The Live view's run dividers now link there (timestamp label becomes an anchor, same markup/classes, no other Live UI change; mirrored in brief.js), and the Ops dashboard's 30-panel in-page run selector is replaced by this page family: /ops/ keeps only the latest run's panel inline, while run selection lives in the Run log history (every row links its run's page; a jump-to <select> navigates the 30 most recent; app.js wireOpsRunPicker is now a navigator, with legacy #run=<id> deep links redirecting). Tests in site/test_build.py § run detail pages.
    • Docs: CLAUDE.md (sub-agent blurb + verification guardrail), README (Phase 5.7 + assurance bullet), docs/architecture.md, docs/routines.md, docs/operating.md (new verification-confirmation signal row), docs/pipeline.md (verification block example shows the confirmation iteration + confirmation_waived).

    What stays

    The rotation itself (odd = Opus cti-verification, even = Sonnet cti-verification-alt), fresh spawn per iteration, the 5-iteration cap, the low-residual early exit (it publishes on a NEEDS_FIXES final with residuals logged, the double-CLEAN gate governs only the CLEAN path), check_run.py green between iterations, ≤3 follow-up research sub-agents per iteration, the 30-min verifier cap, F1–F18, the return contract, and verification_residual_count semantics, all unchanged. Verification still never blocks the run record. Pre-v3.23 records stay green (version-gated enforcement, same pattern as v3.18/v3.21). The Live view's UI is unchanged beyond the run label becoming a link; day pages and briefbook.json shapes are untouched.

    3.22, 2026-07-12 (weekly quality-audit routine: the 2026-07-11 full-store audit institutionalized as a scheduled run, with the monthly priority-calibration review folded in)

    Why

    Operator-directed. The 2026-07-11 full-store intelligence-quality audit (docs/audits/2026-07-11-intelligence-quality-audit.md) found three published factual errors, one clear coverage miss, and five systemic/operational defects (every one real, narrow, and traceable to a fixable mechanism) but it only ran because a human asked. Continuous improvement needs the audit to be a standing routine, not an event: a weekly pass that re-verifies the trailing window's published entries against primary sources (soundness), independently re-researches the window and diffs it against the store (completeness), reviews the machinery for drift (runaway runs, dark-but-green sources, discipline decay), re-checks the previous audit's watch items, and confirms its shipped fixes actually took. The audit's recommendation 3, a monthly pass over the priority distribution (the high share sat at 37 %, stable but on the generous side) against verifier F16 priority-calibration drift, to keep the notification channel honest, is folded into the same routine rather than becoming a fourth one. (Recommendations 1, org-profile watchlists, and 2, scheduler-side watchdog, are operator-side and addressed separately.)

    What changed

    • New master prompt prompts/quality-audit.md: the weekly quality-audit run. Builds on cti-run.md exactly like the weekly does (runtime Read, shared machinery defined once, this file carries only the audit lens). Shape: Phase 0 preflight with a self-healing window anchored on the previous -audit run record (default 7 days, 21-day cap, 72-h duplicate-audit guard mirroring duplicate-week), carry-forward of open watch items and fix-effectiveness duties from prior reports, and the monthly-calibration duty check; Phase 1 retrospective truth passes (batched ≤20 entries, cti-verification/-alt rotation for model diversity, 45-min research-class cap, per-CVE-authority / verbatim-quote / pinned-ATT&CK / classification checks); Phase 2 independent coverage re-sweeps (cti-research G1 vulns / G2 incidents + watch-item corroboration / G3 research-blog listing sweeps, the discovery path behind both 07-11 misses); Phase 3 systemic review (telemetry, publish follow-through, reachable-but-unreadable sources, discipline drift, gate/pin state, previous-fix effectiveness); Phase 3b monthly priority-calibration review (first fire of each calendar month, keyed on the absence of a ## Priority calibration section in the month's audit reports; F16 drift vs fixed bars; calibration edits only against concrete mis-prioritized entries); Phase 4 root-cause-and-fix with the repair discipline made explicit (in-place repair ONLY for the logged immutability-exception class, machine-surface metadata; everything else update_of or recommendation; recovered entries pass the full normal gates); Phase 5 audit report under docs/audits/ mirroring the 07-11 structure incl. carried-forward recommendations and watch items; Phases 5.5→7 verbatim from cti-run.md, with the Phase 5.7 verifier scope extended to the audit report itself (the 07-11 audit's iteration 1 caught exactly that class). New invariants A-INV-1…5 (immutability carve-out bounded, no manufactured findings, report+record ship even cut-short, versioning rule on fixes, never race the scheduled runs).
    • Run-record contract: run_id = <date>T<HHMM>Z-audit, kind: intel (per the 07-11 precedent; RUN_KINDS unchanged), retrospective passes recorded under sub_agents: telemetry, entries_published counts only audit-recovered entries.
    • docs/routines.md § 1d + § 2 table row; docs/operating.md § Set up the routines item 3: the routine invocation prompt (Read prompts/quality-audit.md and execute it.), cadence guidance, and catalog entry.
    • CLAUDE.md: quality-audit prompt added to the repo map, the versioning-rule file list, and the shared-machinery note (an edit to a cti-run.md phase contract now also re-checks the audit prompt's references).
    • Banner bumps: prompts/cti-run.md + prompts/weekly-summary.md + prompts/quality-audit.md → v3.22 in lockstep (no body change to the first two).

    What stays

    Every intel-run and weekly phase contract, PD-1…13, W-PD-1, the inclusion gate, dedup polarity, the verifier loop, the publishing chain, all untouched: the audit is additive and read-mostly. Entry immutability stays a hard invariant; the audit does not widen the 07-11 exception class, it bounds it (A-INV-1). The priority bars themselves (critical extreme, high TL;DR-worthy) are explicitly fixed reference points the calibration review tunes adherence to, never the bars. tools/check_run.py, site/, and the content model need no changes; the audit run record uses the existing kind: intel shape.

    3.21, 2026-07-11 (full-store quality-audit hardening: CVE-id provenance, dead-ATT&CK-id gate FAIL, main-run watchdog, outage-backfill research-blog sweep)

    Why

    Operator-directed full-store intelligence audit (three verification passes over all 55 entries of 2026-07-08…-11 against their primary sources; three independent landscape re-sweeps of 2026-07-03…-11). Findings: (1) one entry propagated three wrong wolfSSL CVE ids from a Talos roundup blog that contradicted Talos's own per-CVE advisory pages, a wrong id poisons the CVE dedup index, the /cve/ surfaces and automated triage matching; (2) one entry shipped a CVSS 9.9 for a CVE the vendor advisory scores 8.5, inverting the cluster's severity ranking; (3) one entry shipped a revoked ATT&CK id (T1656) that the pinned dataset already marked revoked at compose time, the gate only WARNed; (4) two runs in one week silently ran 11.2 h and 17.8 h wall-clock, publishing hours late and being overtaken by the next fire (one overtaken run improvised the correct re-sync-and-dedup recovery, codify it); (5) the 62 h scheduler-outage backfill run caught every KEV/CERT item but missed two vendor research-blog publications dated inside the gap (Kaspersky Armored Likho/BusySnake, government + electric-power targeting; Infoblox Lurking Lizard) because research blogs do not route through CVE/KEV discovery paths.

    What changed

    • Phase 2 step 4, CVE-id provenance rule: a CVE id and its CVSS are transcribed from the record that owns them (per-CVE advisory, vendor PSIRT, discloser's per-vulnerability page, e.g. a TALOS advisory's "Vendor Response (CVE-…)" field), never from a multi-CVE roundup alone; on disagreement the per-CVE authority wins and the discrepancy goes in sourcing_note; an id resolving nowhere never enters cves[].
    • Phase 4 § ATT&CK / gate: unknown, revoked, or deprecated techniques[] ids now FAIL tools/check_run.py on v3.21+ runs (MAPPING_IDS_STRICT_FROM); the pin is on disk at compose time, so a dead id is a composition defect; store-wide (--all) stays WARN because a later pin update revoking a previously-active id is legitimate drift on immutable history.
    • New anti-crash guard #10, main-run wall-clock watchdog: check elapsed time at every phase boundary; past ~3 h, stop widening, land the already-verified candidates through gate → one verifier iteration → publish, and record the overrun. When a later fire overtook the run, re-fetch origin/main, rebuild prior-coverage, and re-dedup before composing (the 2026-07-09T2009Z recovery, now codified). check_run.py WARNs on duration_seconds > 3 h (per-run and --all).
    • Phase 0 step 6, outage-backfill duty: when gap_hours > 24, S3's spawn message gains an explicit per-publisher research-blog listing sweep for the outage dates before normal in-window work.
    • tools/check_run.py: the three gates above, plus --all WARN on a v3.14+ run record still carrying no publish_status >24 h after it started (a Phase 7 amendment that never landed, observed on 2026-07-09T1211Z).
    • Verifier F2 scope note (both definitions, byte-identical): frontmatter cves[] ids/scores are cross-checked against the per-CVE authority, not just the entry's own cited roundup.
    • Store repairs under the immutability-exception log (.claude/memory/entry-immutability-exceptions.md): wolfSSL CVE ids corrected (CVE-2026-28739→7532, 25106→5263, 33091→6678; state/cves_seen.json re-synced), BeyondTrust CVE-2026-40141 CVSS 9.9→8.5, Odido T1656→T1684.001. Audit report: docs/audits/2026-07-11-intelligence-quality-audit.md.
    • sources/sources.json ncsc-uk: working discovery recipe recorded (all-rss-feed.xml is fresh; the HTML listing returns a consent-banner shell to every transport); an essential source had been dark-but-green for weeks because reachability was mistaken for readability.

    What stays

    Every phase contract, the inclusion gate (PD-11), dedup polarity, entry immutability (repairs are logged exceptions, not precedent), the verifier loop, and the weekly's divergent lens, the weekly inherits these edits via its runtime read of cti-run.md; its banner moves to v3.21 in lockstep.

    3.20, 2026-07-11 (typed entity relationships replace the untyped related list; interactive threat graph at /graph/)

    Why

    The registry's related: [] was a flat, untyped, direction-less key list: it could say two entities were connected but never how ("Akira → Groupe 3R" could be attribution, tooling, or coincidence) or on what evidence. That capped what the entity pages, dedup reasoning, and any downstream consumer could do with the graph, and it left analysts with no way to visually investigate how an actor, a campaign, a CVE and a piece of tooling hang together. Operator-directed rework: make every relationship typed, directed, and evidence-bound, and give analysts a graph exploration surface over the whole store.

    What changed

    • relations[] replaces related (no backward compatibility). Each edge: {to, type, source, note}, a controlled 10-type vocabulary (attributed-to, uses, exploits, part-of, variant-of, successor-of, collaborates-with, overlaps-with, documented-in, related-to) with per-type direction and endpoint constraints (content_model.RELATION_TYPES); source is the entry id whose cited reporting establishes the edge (REQUIRED; every curated edge is evidence-bound and dated by its entry). Normative: docs/pipeline.md § Relationships. All legacy edges migrated to typed, sourced form (81 → 71 canonical-direction edges after mirror-dedup; 2 dropped for having no in-store evidence); validate_registry FAILs a leftover related, an unknown type, an endpoint-constraint violation, a tombstone endpoint, a duplicate edge, or an unresolvable source entry; check_run.py WARNs when a source entry neither keys nor names an endpoint.
    • Derived edges are first-class and explicit. Entry co-occurrence, entity↔CVE, and entity↔technique connections are computed at render time, always carrying their supporting entries, the two edge classes (curated = source-stated; derived = store-implied) are kept visually and semantically distinct everywhere.
    • /graph/ + data/graph.json: self-contained (strict-CSP, no external libraries) interactive canvas over all canonical entities, connected CVEs and mapped ATT&CK techniques (toggleable layer): type filters, search, hover neighbourhoods, node detail panel with per-edge provenance, drag/pin, neighbourhood isolation, shortest-path tracing between two pinned nodes, ?focus=/?to= deep links, server-rendered most-connected directory as the no-JS fallback. Entity pages render typed relations grouped by reading ("uses", "attributed activity", …) with source-entry links, plus the derived co-occurring list, and link into the graph.
    • Phase 4 § Entity linking / Phase 5 § registry rewritten for the typed model: relate only what a source states, overlaps-with never upgraded beyond the claim, duplicate edges never added, evolved relationships update type/source/note in place, tombstoning moves edges to the canonical record.
    • Phrase-attachment fixes in site/build.py: word-boundary confirmation (a substring match like "Lace" inside "necklace" no longer attaches an entity and pollutes co-occurrence) and case-sensitive short-acronym matching ("INC", "CRA", "888" are no longer unmatchable in prose).
    • Research-agent contract: relation_suggestions (.claude/agents/cti-research.md § Entity registry + findings-YAML skeleton), when a fetched source explicitly states a connection between tracked entities, the sub-agent returns {subject, object, basis} with the claiming source; the main agent maps suggestions onto the typed vocabulary and owns the registry write. Suggest-only, never inferred, the same claim-attribution discipline as everywhere else.

    What stays

    Everything else: the entity registry as the single entity namespace, permanent keys, alias discipline, merged_into tombstone semantics, CVEs never registry entities, the ATT&CK derivation layer, and every other phase contract. The relationship rework changes how connections are recorded and explored, not what gets covered or how it is verified. Entries untouched (immutability intact); the weekly file needs no edit, it inherits Phase 4/5 via its runtime read of cti-run.md, and its banner moves to v3.20 in lockstep.

    3.19, 2026-07-11 (action items get a do-now bar: fewer, concrete, finding-derived; empty is the normal case, quality over quantity across the whole brief)

    Why

    Operator-directed. The rendered brief's § Action Items is the union of every in-window entry's actions[], and with each entry shipping 2–5 actions a ~16-entry day produced a 40–60-item task list; far too long for an on-shift team to actually work, and diluted with items that were not tasks at all: standing detection-engineering ideas, general hardening dressed in product names, and restatements of the body's detection guidance. A list nobody can finish is a list nobody starts, the genuinely urgent patch-and-terminate item drowns between technique-class hunting suggestions and process reminders. The operator's direction: action items must be only what the team should immediately do, concrete and derived from the specific finding, and it is explicitly fine for an entry to carry none; the same quality-over-quantity discipline applies to the whole brief, complete over the relevant signal, but never overloading the analyst, because a finding that isn't analysed in detail might as well not have been published.

    What changed

    • New Phase 4 § actions[], the do-now bar (prompts/cti-run.md). An action ships only when ALL hold: (1) concrete and self-contained, exact product / version boundary / config surface / log source from the entry's own cited facts, executable without re-reading the entry; hedged phrasing ("consider", "where applicable") disqualifies; (2) derived from this finding, not from good practice; the test is "would this sentence be equally true if this entry had never been published?"; yes ⇒ generic advice, never an action, not even dressed in product names; (3) do-now urgency, started this shift/week (patch/mitigate an exploited exposure, terminate/rotate what the mechanics say is compromised, a bounded compromise-check for the specific artifact); standing detection ideas and open-ended technique-class hunts are body content. Zero actions is the correct output for a large share of entries (awareness/research, out-of-nexus lesson entries, most updates); more than three on one entry is near-certain body restatement; no verbatim repeats of an earlier in-window entry's action. The frontmatter bullet now points at the section; the quality-gate checklist gains an actions[] line.
    • Inclusion and action items decoupled (PD-11 calibration): the borderline call's "yes ⇒ include" no longer implies an actions[] line; what the responder would do differently lives in the body's **Defender takeaway:**, and reaches actions[] only when it clears the do-now bar. Added the attention-economics rationale: analyst attention is the resource the brief spends; soundness (no marginal item, no generic action) protects it, completeness (no missing relevant item) earns it; the fix for overload is always to cut the marginal, never the relevant.
    • Verifier: new finding category F18 action-item-discipline (editorial): new check 10b + report section + verdict-count and slug updates in .claude/agents/cti-verification.md; flags generic advice, body restatement, hedged non-tasks, in-window duplicates, and padded lists (> ~3), with an explicit "an empty actions[] is never a defect; never ask for an action to be added". cti-verification-alt.md regenerated byte-identical below its header note (both headers now say F1–F18); prompts/verification.md checklist + editorial-gate + F-range updated; docs/routines.md and CLAUDE.md references updated.
    • Mechanical gate: tools/check_run.py gains a WARN-severity action-items check for v3.19+ runs, flags >3 actions on one entry, canonical generic-advice phrases ("enable MFA", "patch regularly", …), and verbatim duplicate actions across the run's entries. WARN, not FAIL: genericity is an editorial judgment (F18 owns it); the mechanical check only surfaces the textbook cases. Pre-v3.19 records are exempt (entries are immutable).
    • prompts/entry-template.md: skeleton now shows actions: [] as the default with the do-now bar in the comment, instead of a filled example implying every entry carries actions.
    • New CLAUDE.md hard rule (ALWAYS hold actions[] to the do-now bar; empty is the normal case) so every session, routine or interactive, carries the discipline.
    • Banner bumps: prompts/cti-run.md + prompts/weekly-summary.md → v3.19 in lockstep (the weekly inherits the bar for any operational-kind entries it produces; its strategic lens is unchanged).

    What stays

    The inclusion gate itself (PD-11) is untouched in both directions: the brief stays sound AND complete, no marginal item survives, no relevant item is dropped, and there is still no entry count, target, or ceiling anywhere; this change governs what an included entry puts into actions[], never whether it is included. Bodies keep their full detection / hunting / hardening depth (Detection clause, **Defender takeaway:**, **Triage:**, that is where cut action content goes, not into the void). No published entry was edited (immutability intact), the pre-v3.19 store keeps its historical action lists and stays green under --all. The critical/immediate_action bar, deep-dive selection, dedup polarity, triage-ready composition rules, ATT&CK mapping discipline, classification rules, and all hard invariants: unchanged.

    3.18, 2026-07-10 (always rated, always mapped: no entry ships without a classification or, on behavior kinds, an ATT&CK mapping; gate FAILs enforce it)

    Why

    Operator-directed. The NATO Admiralty classification and the ATT&CK mapping are the two signals a reader (human or triage agent) uses to judge how much to trust an item and what behavior it describes, yet both were structurally optional. Because this deployment configures no vulnerability-triage scheme, every vulnerability entry fell through the triage-kind exemption and shipped with NEITHER rating (classification: null + org_triage: null); the exemption was designed for a scheme that doesn't exist here, leaving the most action-driving entry kind permanently unrated. And techniques[] was allowed to be "empty when the entry genuinely maps none" with only a WARN behind it, although a threat/incident/vulnerability entry always has a mappable access or exploitation vector; an empty mapping on those kinds is a composition defect, not a judgment call, and it leaves the entity TTP profiles, the /attack/ matrix and the Navigator exports blind to that entry. WARN-severity enforcement means drift accumulates silently; the operator's direction is that every future report and every derived entity profile carries both signals, always, mechanically guaranteed.

    What changed

    • Policy: the triage-kind exemption exists only while a triage scheme does. When the org profile configures vulnerability_triage.categories, triage kinds carry org_triage + classification: null exactly as before. When none is configured (this deployment), triage kinds carry the Admiralty classification block like every other kind, the Admiralty code rates the reporting (vendor PSIRT = A, credibility from corroboration), which fits a vulnerability disclosure exactly. Every entry ships with exactly one rating, never zero. tools/compose_prompts.py renders the deployment-resolved rule into the org-data blocks (both master prompts + research agent) and the verifier context, with selftest coverage for both branches; prompts/cti-run.md § Org-triage / § Intel classification and prompts/entry-template.md state the fallback.
    • Policy: behavior kinds always map. threat / incident / vulnerability entries must carry a non-empty, evidence-bound techniques[], at minimum the access or exploitation vector (exposed-service RCE → T1190, phishing lure → T1566, LPE → T1068, …); research / annual-report should map unless the piece genuinely carries no TTP content; policy / synthesis / outlook may map nothing. Completeness of evidence-supported mappings, never invention, every id still names a behavior the body describes and a source supports. Phase 4 § Triage-ready rule 2, the frontmatter contract, the quality-gate checklist, hard invariant 17, prompts/entry-template.md and the research agent's findings-YAML contract all state it.
    • Mechanical gate (tools/check_run.py), version-gated at v3.18: for runs whose prompt_version ≥ 3.18: a missing rating (classification on required kinds, org_triage on scheme-owned triage kinds, incl. unknown category ids) and an empty techniques[] on a behavior-kind entry are FAILs; pre-3.18 records keep WARN severity (entries are immutable; history stays green, the future is gated). Out-of-vocabulary codes remain FAILs at any version. --all gains a store-ratings sweep that re-applies the enforced checks to every entry of every v3.18+ run forever, so the whole-store validation permanently guarantees "everything is classified". New fix recipes in prompts/check-run-fixes.md (classification missing-rating, org-triage, attack-mapping empty-techniques rows).
    • Verifier (F11/F17 scope, both definitions in lockstep): F17 restated around "every entry carries exactly one rating, never zero" with the deployment-resolved rule read from § Organization context; check 4b flags an empty techniques[] on a behavior-kind entry as F11 (gate-bypass safety net). cti-verification-alt.md regenerated byte-identical below its header note.
    • prompts/verification.md: quality-gate checklist lines for classification (never zero ratings) and techniques (never empty on behavior kinds) updated to the same rule.
    • Banner bumps: prompts/cti-run.md + prompts/weekly-summary.md → v3.18 in lockstep (the weekly inherits everything; its weekly-vuln-rollup entries are vulnerability-kind and now carry the Admiralty block; strategic kinds keep their exemptions).

    What stays

    No published entry was edited (immutability intact), the 894-entry pre-3.18 store keeps its historical rating coverage and stays green under --all (WARN at most). The rating vocabularies, the Admiralty axes-independence rule, the evidence-bound mapping discipline ("omitted, never invented" for anything the sources don't support), the F16/F17 truthfulness scope, the configured-scheme behavior (org_triage owns triage kinds the moment a scheme is added to the config), the pinned-dataset lifecycle, and all twenty hard invariants: unchanged. A deployment that configures a triage scheme in config/org-profile.yaml gets the original split back automatically via compose_prompts.py --write; the fallback is a property of the empty config, not a removal of the scheme.

    3.17, 2026-07-09 (versioned MITRE ATT&CK layer: pinned dataset, entity/CVE TTP derivation, Navigator-style overlap matrix, ATT&CK-in-metadata-not-prose)

    Why

    Operator-directed. The pipeline talked about ATT&CK without being mapped to it: techniques[] existed in the schema (v3 data model) but zero of 888 published entries carried it, every mapping lived as inline T-ids in prose, invisible to machine consumers, entity pages, and any TTP-level analysis. There was no pinned ATT&CK release to validate ids against (v19 renamed Defense Evasion to Stealth + Defense Impairment and added TA0112, ids and tactics genuinely drift), no way to see an actor's or vulnerability's technique profile, and no way to compare TTP overlap between actors the way an ATT&CK Navigator layer does. Editorially the old rule pushed ids into prose ("woven, never listed"), which cluttered the brief text with numbers most readers skim past while still leaving the machine layer empty, the operator's direction inverts it: keep ATT&CK out of the running text unless essential, make the metadata complete, and derive everything else from there.

    What changed

    • Pinned, versioned ATT&CK dataset: new attack/enterprise-attack.json (contract: attack/README.md), a compact extraction (technique id → name, tactics, first-paragraph definition, sub-technique parentage, platforms, lifecycle flags with revoked_by forwarding, the ATT&CK analogue of registry tombstones; revoked/deprecated ids are kept so immutable entries keep resolving) from the official mitre-attack/attack-stix-data STIX releases, pinned at v19.1. New tools/attack_data.py owns its lifecycle: --check (local vs upstream latest via the official index.json), --update [--version X.Y] (rewrite + printed change summary for the commit body), --selftest (offline invariants), --info.
    • Derived, evidence-bound TTP mappings: site/content_model.py gains load_attack_dataset / resolve_technique_id / entry_technique_ids (frontmatter ∪ in-prose T-ids, dataset-filtered, revoked-forwarded; prose extraction is permanent because the 888-entry legacy store is immutable and carries its mappings in prose only). site/build.py aggregates per entity AND per CVE: technique → supporting entry ids, never an assertion without a published entry behind it.
    • Site surfaces: entity/CVE pages gain an ATT&CK section (techniques grouped by tactic in official matrix order, definitions from the pin, evidence links, lifecycle badges) + a per-entity ATT&CK Navigator layer export (entities/<key>/attack-layer.json, layer format 4.5, importable into the official Navigator). New /attack/ page: the full enterprise matrix, cells heat-shaded by store-wide coverage, a per-technique definitions-and-evidence directory (no-JS fallback), and a client-side multi-entity overlap view (assets/js/attack.js + data/attack.json) with union / overlap≥2 / common-to-all modes, per-entity color dots, URL-shareable selections (?sel=…), and Navigator-layer export of the current comparison. techniques[] added to data/briefbook.json + data/alerts.json records; covered techniques joined the search index; ATT&CK link in the site nav.
    • Mechanical gate: tools/check_run.py FAILs a missing/invariant-violating dataset (attack-dataset) and WARNs (attack-mapping) on techniques[] ids unknown/revoked/deprecated in the pin and (run scope only) on prose-mapped ids missing from techniques[].
    • Editorial inversion (prompts/cti-run.md → v3.17): Phase 4 rule 2 rewritten from "ATT&CK woven, never listed" to "ATT&CK in metadata; prose only where essential": techniques[] is the canonical, complete mapping surface (active ids per the pin); prose describes behavior in plain language and must read complete without T-numbers; inline ids only where they earn their place (deep-dive kill chains, mapping-as-the-finding, searched-by terms of art); bare ID lists remain a defect, as does a frontmatter id with no described behavior. Frontmatter contract + quality-gate checklist updated to match. prompts/entry-template.md worked example + skeleton comments aligned. .claude/agents/cti-research.md § Technical depth updated (active ids from the pin; composer keeps ids in metadata). prompts/weekly-summary.md → v3.17 in lockstep + new weekly-only maintenance duty: attack_data.py --check every weekly fire, result in the run record, update-or-surface on drift.

    What stays

    No published entry was edited (immutability intact); legacy prose mappings are derived, not migrated. TECHNIQUE_ID_RE format validation unchanged (still a FAIL); the new dataset checks are WARNs except dataset presence/integrity. Verifier definitions (F1–F17) untouched; frontmatter⇔body agreement was already F-scoped, and the mechanical mirror check is check_run.py's job. No-IOC rule, two-source verification, relevance gate, dedup ladder, entity-registry namespace: unchanged. ORG-PROFILE blocks untouched (banner-only lockstep bump).

    3.16, 2026-07-09 (entity-registry graph overhaul: uniform naming convention, merged_into tombstones, curated related edges)

    Why

    Operator-directed review of the full entity registry (376 records) found systemic quality drift that undermined the registry's purpose as the pipeline's threat graph: name fields carrying reporting-vendor names instead of entity names ("Netcraft", "Symantec", "Check Point" as campaign names), headline sentences truncated mid-word as names, aliases packed into name strings while aliases: [] stayed empty (breaking both dedup alias-matching and the site's phrase-based entry↔entity attachment), nexus null on publicly-attributed APTs, duplicate entities (the same actor/campaign registered twice under different keys, e.g. The Gentlemen as two actor records, the Klue/Icarus Salesforce breach as two campaigns), and clear-cut regulatory items typed campaign:/incident:. There was also no supported way to merge duplicates (keys are permanent, entries immutable) or to record actor↔campaign↔tool↔incident relations the co-occurrence engine can't see.

    What changed

    • Data model (docs/pipeline.md § Entity registry, entities/README.md): two optional registry fields: merged_into: <canonical-key> turns a duplicate record into a tombstone (key stays resolvable for the immutable entries that reference it; new entries must use the canonical key; no chains; tombstones exempt from the alias-collision check), and related: [] carries curated, evidence-bound graph edges rendered symmetrically on entity pages. Plus an explicit uniform naming convention: name = concise canonical entity name only; every alternate public name in aliases; summary = 1–3-sentence attributed English definition.
    • site/content_model.py: resolve_entity_key() helper; validate_registry enforces merged_into / related semantics (existing canonical targets, no chains, no self-references) and skips tombstones in the collision check.
    • site/build.py: tombstones never phrase-match; explicit entry references resolve through tombstones so the canonical entity's page carries the full story timeline; tombstone permalinks stay live as stubs pointing at the canonical entity; entity pills / detail chips / JSON-LD resolve to canonical; /entities/ index hides tombstones; curated related edges merge (symmetric, linked badge) into the co-occurrence-derived Related-entities block and survive its top-8 cap.
    • tools/check_run.py: cross-run dedup resolves entity keys through tombstones (an old entry's tombstoned key and a new entry's canonical key register as the same entity).
    • prompts/cti-run.md: banner → v3.16. § Entity linking + Phase 5 registry lifecycle: naming convention pointer, never reference a tombstone in new entries, related maintenance (append-only, evidence-bound). prompts/weekly-summary.md → v3.16 in lockstep (no weekly divergence; entity linking is shared machinery).
    • entities/registry.yaml: full editorial pass over all records applying the convention (names, aliases, nexus, summaries), duplicate merges (tombstones for referenced keys, deletion for orphans), type corrections (orphan keys renamed; referenced mistyped keys tombstoned to correctly-typed successors), and curated related edges for the established clusters (ShinyHunters, TeamPCP/Shai-Hulud, The Gentlemen, Nightmare Eclipse, Fox Tempest, Kimsuky, MuddyWater, Gamaredon, and others).

    What stays

    Registry keys referenced by any published entry remain resolvable forever; no entry was edited (immutability intact). schema: 1 unchanged; both new fields are optional and additive, so pre-3.16 registry shapes stay valid. The dedup ladder, entity-linking hard rule (one key per real-world entity), mechanical gate, verifier loop, and all hard invariants unchanged. F1–F17, verifier bodies, and ORG-PROFILE blocks untouched.

    3.15, 2026-07-09 (pin-aware sub-agent self-identification: harness-injected prompt line replaces container-scoped env vars as the primary source)

    Why

    The operator flagged that the Ops dashboard's run detail showed every sub-agent of run 2026-07-09T1211Z as "Claude Opus 4.8", S1–S4 research workers (definition pinned model: sonnet) and all three verification iterations including the even-iteration cti-verification-alt slot (pinned sonnet), and was "quite sure they are Sonnet". They were right. The v3.12 protocol made the container env vars CLAUDE_FRIENDLY_NAME/CLAUDE_MODEL_ID the authoritative self-ID source with a documented blind spot: they carry the main-agent default and cannot see a definition's model: frontmatter pin, so every pinned sub-agent dutifully reported Opus and the dashboard rendered a false uniformity (v3.12 had stopped the worse failure (publishing "rotation failed" as fact) but left the misleading model labels themselves). An interactive-session experiment (2026-07-09) resolved what v3.12 had declared unresolvable from inside the sandbox: the harness injects a per-agent model line into each agent's own system prompt (You are powered by the model named <friendly>. The exact model ID is <id>.), generated at spawn time from the same resolution that applies the definition's pin. Diagnostic probes of all three definitions confirmed it is pin-aware: cti-research → "Sonnet 5" / claude-sonnet-5, cti-verification → "Opus 4.8 (1M context)" / claude-opus-4-8[1m], cti-verification-alt → "Sonnet 5" / claude-sonnet-5, while the container env vars uniformly reported "Claude Opus 4.8" / claude-opus-4-8 in the same spawns. Side finding: the probes also confirm pinning and the Phase 5.7 rotation genuinely work.

    What changed

    • .claude/agents/cti-research.md, .claude/agents/cti-verification.md + -alt (body regenerated byte-identically): § Self-identification reordered: primary source is the harness-injected model line in the agent's own system prompt (quoted verbatim, never the name the pin would be "expected" to resolve to); Fallback 1 is the container env vars, now reported with a mandatory provenance marker — container default, env fallback inside the Model-line parentheses (exact shape specified) and never as proof of runtime model; Fallback 2 stays Anthropic Claude (specific model not determined). The verifier iteration-rotation note now says "self-identify from your own harness-injected prompt line, not from the env vars". The stale explanation tying the Model-line fields to the env vars is rewritten to the prompt line.
    • prompts/cti-run.md: banner → v3.15. § Self-identification: main agent likewise identifies from its own prompt line first (for the main agent the env vars do describe the right thing, but they are demoted to marked fallback); the "sub-agent reports are container-scoped" paragraph is replaced by "sub-agent Model lines are pin-aware since v3.15, read the provenance": differing models across sub-agents are the expected healthy signal of pinning/rotation working; only an env-fallback-marked report is blind to the pin, and only such reports keep the uniformity-is-a-measurement-limitation rule (never report a rotation failure as fact from them). Phase 5.7 rotation caveat updated to match (expect the reported model to alternate across iterations). Execution-environment summary line updated.
    • prompts/weekly-summary.md: banner → v3.15 in lockstep (no weekly-specific divergence; the weekly inherits § Self-identification from cti-run.md).
    • CLAUDE.md, docs/routines.md, docs/architecture.md, docs/operating.md § 5, self-ID guidance updated to prompt-line-primary / env-vars-as-marked-fallback; operating.md reframes the env vars as the fallback layer to keep configured (matched to the main-agent model), no longer "the single highest-confidence way".
    • .claude/memory/model-identity-and-rotation.md: records the experiment, the probe results, and the v3.15 protocol; the v3.12 "unresolvable from inside the sandbox" claim is superseded. MEMORY.md index line updated.

    What stays

    Run records and the Ops dashboard consume the **Model:** line / model + model_id fields unchanged, no schema, content_model.py, check_run.py, or site/build.py change (the dashboard's canonicaliser already resolves "Sonnet 5", claude-sonnet-5, and suffixed forms like "Opus 4.8 (1M context)" / claude-opus-4-8[1m]). Published run records claiming Opus for pinned sub-agents stay immutable; they are measurement artifacts of the superseded protocol, documented in memory, never edited. The never-fabricate rule is intact: an agent with neither prompt line nor env vars still reports the explicit not-determined string, never a training-data guess. Verifier bodies still byte-identical; ORG-PROFILE blocks untouched; F1–F17, gate, rotation schedule, and all hard invariants unchanged.

    3.14, 2026-07-09 (operational hardening: bounded parallel source-health sweep; CISA structured-listing recipes; machine-auditable publish_status; compose-workflow race fix; --all grandfathering)

    Why

    Operator directive to fix the operational items the v3.13 run-record analysis surfaced (and confirmed the 2026-07-07 62 h gap as a real scheduler outage). Four standing problems, all repo-fixable: (1) tools/source_health.py blew every wall-clock budget it was given (observed 2026-06-21, 2026-07-05 weekly, 2026-07-08 first attempt); a fully sequential sweep of 150+ sources where a single RSS source could legally burn ~4 minutes walking feed-suffix guesses, leaving state/source_health.json un-refreshed. (2) The CISA listing recipes probed JS shells: cisa page on /news-events/{cybersecurity-advisories,news} returns only the Drupal filter UI, re-logged as a "recipe gap" run after run, while the same listings sit fully structured in the Drupal RSS endpoints. (3) Publish outcome was ephemeral: Phase 7's publish: ok|main-only|pending line lived only in stdout, no machine-auditable record of whether a fire actually published. (4) check_run.py --all was permanently red: four dangling update_of targets from 2026-05/06 renames FAIL forever because entries are immutable, training the operator to ignore the store gate and masking NEW failures. Plus one CI defect the operator hit on the v3.13 push: compose-profile.yml raced auto-merge-claude.yml (branch merged + deleted before the compose clone → couldn't find remote ref, exit 128), and its paths: filter still watched the v2 filename prompts/daily-cti-brief.md; it had silently not been watching the v3 master prompt at all.

    What changed

    • tools/source_health.py: parallel sweep (--workers, default 10) with an overall wall-clock budget (--budget, default 420 s; 0 = unlimited): live-tested 151/154 sources probed in 45 s vs. the sequential sweep's 320 s+ overruns. On budget exhaustion the snapshot still writes complete: un-probed sources carry the previous snapshot's result forward (carried_forward: true) so latest never silently shrinks; sources with no prior record get class not-probed/action none. Per-source cap (~75 s) on the RSS feed-suffix walk, the historical single-source budget killer. Probe-crash → an unreachable record, never a lost source. CISA probe map switched to the structured feeds (below).
    • CISA structured-listing recipes: sources/sources.json: cisa-advisories gains rss_url: https://www.cisa.gov/cybersecurity-advisories/all.xml, cisa-news gains rss_url: https://www.cisa.gov/news.xml; both notes now name the REQUIRED listing fetch python3 tools/fetch_source.py cisa feed <feed-url> N (structured {title,link} items, the existing cisa feed recipe, now wired in; confirmed working 2026-07-09) with cisa page <link> for the drill. cisa-directives (no feed exists) documents the confirmed-working discovery path: the reader DOES hydrate the directives listing (grep /news-events/directives/<slug> hrefs) and new directives are also announced in news.xml. No tools/fetch_source.py code change was needed; the gap was wiring, not capability. (One-time side effect: sources.json re-normalized to its canonical ensure_ascii=False serialization; ~180 non-ASCII lines had drifted to \uXXXX escapes on an earlier run.)
    • Machine-auditable publish_status: run-record frontmatter gains publish_status: pending | ok | main-only + publish_checked_at + publish_note (optional, absent on pre-v3.14 records; enum enforced by content_model.validate_run_record). prompts/cti-run.md → v3.14: Phase 5 writes pending before the commit; new Phase 7c amends the record in place after the poll, commits run: <run-id> publish-status: <status> and pushes fire-and-forget (one backoff retry, no re-poll, auto-merge promotes it on its own). Phase 0 step 3 + tools/run_summary.py surface runs.last_run.publish_status, so the NEXT fire detects a record stuck pending (fire died pre-Phase 7, or the amendment push failed) and notes it. Hard invariant #19 wording now names the two sanctioned same-fire in-place updates (same-minute retry, 7c amendment), nothing else, never a later fire; docs/pipeline.md § Run records and runs/README.md carry the same contract; CLAUDE.md bullet updated. prompts/weekly-summary.md → v3.14 in lockstep (the weekly executes cti-run.md Phases 5→7 verbatim, so 7c applies unchanged).
    • tools/check_run.py: check_references_resolve (--all) now grades severity by commit state, the same pattern as check_prompt_version: dangling update_of/references on an uncommitted entry still FAILs (that is the moment to fix it); on a committed (immutable) entry it WARNs as grandfathered. --all on the store: 13 pass · 4 warn · 0 fail; the gate is green and meaningful again, and the four historical defects stay visible as WARNs.
    • .github/workflows/compose-profile.yml: clone step now handles the auto-merge race: fetch failed + claude/* branch confirmed gone via ls-remote ⇒ fall back to validating the promoted result on main (COMPOSE_REF=main) with a ::notice::, instead of exit 128; a still-existing branch that fails to fetch remains a hard error. paths: filter and the operator-branch git add corrected prompts/daily-cti-brief.md → prompts/cti-run.md.
    • .github/workflows/source-health.yml: timeout-minutes: 15 outer cap (the sweep itself is now budget-bounded).
    • .claude/memory/scheduler-and-workflow-races.md (new, indexed), the operator-confirmed 2026-07-07 scheduler outage (diagnosis rule: missing runs/<date>/ dir + clean previous fire = scheduler-side; PD-7 self-heals coverage, surface the gap, don't re-diagnose the repo), the compose×auto-merge race + fix, and the sources.json ensure_ascii drift symptom.

    What stays

    Every hard invariant. Entry immutability is untouched (the 7c amendment is a run-record field update by the same fire, entries never change; the four grandfathered dangling links stay visible as WARNs precisely because they can never be edited). The publishing chain is unchanged in shape, feature-branch-only, auto-merge promotes, Phase 7 polls; 7c only records the outcome the poll already produced.     3.13, 2026-07-09 (triage-ready behavioral intelligence: dual-audience entries for humans AND alert-triage agents; techniques[] / affected_products[] machine layer; contiguous-evidence + per-fact-attribution compose rules)

    Why

    Operator directive: make the pipeline's output world-class threat intelligence that is rock-solid, highly actionable, and directly ingestible by AI agents, specifically, an automated alert-triage agent (the agentic-SOC pattern: enrichment → triage agent → specialized forensics agents → final review, matching alerts against a threat-knowledge base) must be able to use the entry store to make informed true/false-positive decisions and recognize the described attacks in logs, and a human defender must be able to do the same; detection guidance must stay vendor-agnostic (behavior and methodology, never product rule code), and bare MITRE ATT&CK ID listings must give way to techniques woven into the behavioral narrative. Two audits back the changes. (1) Entry audit (14 entries, 2026-06-29 → 07-09): native v3 bodies already describe attacks at telemetry-matchable granularity and weave ATT&CK into prose, but the richest triage content is prose-only: 80 distinct technique IDs across the store with no structured frontmatter field, no queryable product field, and benign-lookalike/disambiguation guidance present only in the best entries. An agent can retrieve by CVE/entity/tag but not by technique, product, or behavior; the three axes an alert actually carries. (2) Run-record analysis (83 records, v2.19 → v3.12): the top recurring truth defects are concentrated and prompt-fixable, F3 citation-attribution mismatch (167 findings; facts attributed to the wrong co-cited source), F4 non-verbatim evidence (148; dominated by ellipsis-spliced quotes), F13/F14 unsourced links/quantifiers (39); plus F11 policy-shorthand leaks ("per PD-9") into reader-facing text.

    What changed

    • prompts/cti-run.md: banner → v3.13. New intro block "Two readers, one artifact": every entry serves the human Tier 2/3 responder AND an automated SOC/triage agent ingesting the store as its knowledge base, both matching observed telemetry against the entry; structured frontmatter is the machine retrieval layer, the body the reasoning layer, shape of actionability only, no change to scope/sourcing/gates. New Phase 4 § Triage-ready behavioral description: (1) attack flow as observable behavior, telemetry class named vendor-neutrally first with platform-native anchors as examples only, never rule code; (2) ATT&CK woven, never listed, IDs inline at the behavior they name, bare ID lists are a defect, machine list lives in techniques[], every frontmatter id must match a described behavior; (3) **Triage:** discriminator (benign lookalike + separating features) where the cited mechanism supports one, omitted, never invented, otherwise; (4) derivation discipline; manifestation/discriminator claims must follow mechanically from cited facts (else F4). Frontmatter contract adds techniques[] and affected_products[]. § Compose strictly from the findings files hardened against the measured defect classes: rule 2 extended with never-connect (actor↔campaign↔victim↔tooling links only when a cited source states them, F13); rule 3 covers absolutes (F14); rule 4 defines verbatim as contiguous (no ellipsis splices, no re-hedging; two passages = two records, the top F4 pattern); new rule 5 per-fact source attribution (each atomic fact cited to the specific co-cited source that states it, the top F3 pattern). Style rules ban internal-policy shorthand (PD-numbers, phase names) in ALL reader-facing text including run-record notes (recurring F11). Quality-gates checklist gains a triage-ready box.
    • prompts/weekly-summary.md: banner → v3.13 in lockstep (strategic entries inherit the Phase 4 composition rules unchanged; no weekly-specific divergence needed; techniques[]/affected_products[] are optional fields synthesis entries populate when they carry technique/product content).
    • prompts/entry-template.md: skeleton gains techniques[] + affected_products[] with usage comments and the contiguous-quote comment on evidence[]; the worked-good fragment now demonstrates the full shape (telemetry-class-first detection with platform anchors as examples, IDs woven at the behavior, a **Triage:** discriminator derived from the mechanism) plus a trailing note naming those properties; threat/incident variant documents the Triage line.
    • prompts/verification.md: self-check checklist: evidence quotes contiguous-verbatim; new box for techniques[]/affected_products[] body-support, woven-not-listed IDs, and derive-or-omit Triage lines.
    • .claude/agents/cti-research.md: § Technical depth: ATT&CK bullet now returns mapped ids as the item's techniques: list AND ties each id to the behavior in the summary; defender-takeaway bullet reframed telemetry-class-first (vendor-neutral class, then the platform anchor, portability to any stack and to triage agents); new bullet behavioral manifestation & benign lookalikes (observable sequence + discriminators, only what the source supports or the mechanism dictates). Findings-YAML item schema gains techniques: and affected_products: fields.
    • .claude/agents/cti-verification.md + -alt (body regenerated byte-identically): check 4b: evidence quotes must be contiguous (ellipsis-splice/re-hedge = F4); techniques[] ids must name a described, source-supported behavior (unsupported id = F4; clearly-mapped-but-missing id = F11); affected_products[] values must be source-named.
